2. J -> G -> H -> I -> P
3. J -> R -> Q -> P

### Minimum Node Removal:

- Remove G and any one of I or H.
- Remove H and any one of I or G.

1. Removing G: This will disconnect the path J -> G -> H -> I -> Q -> P and J -> G -> H -> I -> P.
2. Removing H: This will disconnect the path J -> G -> H -> I -> Q -> P and J -> G -> H -> I -> P.
3. Removing I: This will disconnect the paths J -> G -> H -> I -> Q -> P and J -> G -> H -> I -> P, and J -> R -> Q -> I -> P.
4. Removing Q: This will disconnect the paths J -> G -> H -> I -> Q -> P and J -> R -> Q -> P.
5. Removing R: This will disconnect the paths J -> G -> H -> I -> Q -> P and J -> R -> Q -> P.

The smallest cut set involves removing nodes such that all paths are disrupted. The minimal cut set is achieved by removing:

     - \(A \rightarrow F \rightarrow T \rightarrow B \rightarrow G \rightarrow H \rightarrow I\)

   - Through E:
     - \(A \rightarrow E \rightarrow S \rightarrow B \rightarrow G \rightarrow H \rightarrow I\)
     - \(A \rightarrow E \rightarrow S \rightarrow B \rightarrow G \rightarrow C \rightarrow N \rightarrow R \rightarrow O \rightarrow Q \rightarrow J \rightarrow H \rightarrow I\)

   - We observe that node B is a key bridge connecting \(A\) and \(I\). If we remove the connection between \(B\) and \(G\), it disrupts several paths to \(I\).

   - \(B \rightarrow T\)
   - \(B \rightarrow G\)
   - and either \(G \rightarrow H\) or \(G \rightarrow C\)

By removing the three edges \((B, G), (B, T), (G, H)\), we ensure that all paths between \(A\) and \(I\) are disrupted.

To determine the minimum number of edges to remove so that the two black nodes (I and R) are completely disconnected, we need to identify all paths between them and remove the minimum number of edges that disrupt all these paths.

### Paths from I to R:
1. I -> J -> R
2. I -> C -> J -> R
3. I -> H -> J -> R
4. I -> G -> J -> R
5. I -> T -> J -> R
6. I -> B -> P -> J -> R
7. I -> B -> F -> J -> R
8. I -> B -> E -> J -> R
9. I -> B -> S -> J -> R
10. I -> B -> N -> J -> R
11. I -> Q -> J -> R

### Cut Set Analysis:
To disconnect I and R, we need to remove edges from the above paths such that no path exists between I and R.

We break down potential cut sets to find the minimum number:

1. **Removing (J, R):**
   - This removes all direct paths via J but does not solve for paths through F/G/T where those nodes connect to other nodes that connect elsewhere.

2. **Removing (I, J) and (R, J):**
   - This would remove all paths passing through J, but those do not cover other potential multi paths through F/G/T/H where those connect through B or C links.

3. **Removing (I, J), (J, R) and (B, P):**
   - This covers paths to J and other paths through P/B loops which are key to keeping separate path partitions.

A minimal cut set in this case needs all direct and intermediate paths covered:
- Removing (I, J) disrupt I-direct connection.
- Removing (R, J) ensures J-R direction removed.
- Removing (B, P) ensures B-P-J-R path disrupted and separate each disjoint paths further.

Thus the resulting minimal cut set will be removing 3 edges:

**Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(I, J), (J, R), (B, P)]**
